## CHAPTER II.

->*Wherein is declared that the Grecians*<-

After that a Son of Noah <note>Referring to the biblical division of the world among Shem, Ham, and Japheth after the Great Flood.</note> possessed the third part of the world after the Flood, this Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy or the Hermetic secrets of nature.</note> broke in violently, as it were, into Chaldea and Persia, and from there into Egypt. This Art being slightly sensed by the superstitious and idolatrous Greeks, some among them who were more wise and sage betook themselves to the Chaldeans and Egyptians, so that they might draw the same wisdom out of their Schools. But now, whereas the Theological or Bible-study of the Law of Moses did not so well please them, they confided in their own proper Genius <note>original: "Genius" - here meaning their own natural intellect or spirit rather than divine revelation.</note>, and fell away from the right foundation of those natural secrets and arts. This is evident by their fabulous Conceptions and stumblings about the doctrine of Moses. It was the Egyptians' custom to propose those Traditions of that so excellent wisdom merely in Enigmatical figures <note>Riddles or symbolic images intended to hide the truth from the uninitiated.</note>—

## CHAP. III.
### *What was taught in the Schools of the Egyptians.*

THE Chaldeans, Persians and Egyptians had [all of them] the same knowledge of the secrets of nature and the same Religion, the names only being changed. The Chaldeans & Persians called their doctrine *Wisdom* <note>original: "Sophia"</note> and *Magick*, and the Egyptians, because of the sacrifice, called their wisdom the Priest-hood. The Magick of the Persians, and Theology of the Egyptians were both of them heretofore taught in the Schools. Albeit there were many Schools and Learned men in *Arabia, Africa & Greece*, as *Albumazar, Abenzagel, Geber, Rasis* and *Avicen* <note>These are Latinized names for famous Islamic scholars like Al-Kindi, Jabir ibn Hayyan, Al-Razi, and Ibn Sina.</note>, amongst the Arabians. *Machaon, Podalirius, Pythagoras, Anaxagoras, Democritus, Plato, Aristotle* and *Rodianus* amongst the Grecians; but yet there were various opinions amongst themselves as to the Egyptian

It lists influential Arabian and Greek philosophers who inherited these traditions despite their differing opinions.</summary>

<keywords>Egyptian Schools, Magick, Sophia, Persian Wisdom, Arabian Philosophers, Greek Philosophers, Alchemy, History of Science</keywords>

For this cause *Pythagoras* would not be called a Sophist <note>In this context, "Sophist" refers to a "wise man" or professor of wisdom, though it later took on a negative connotation of deceptive reasoning.</note>, because the Egyptian Priesthood and Wisdom was not at all perfectly taught as was fitting, although he received thence many Mysteries and Arcanums <note>term: Arcanum (a secret or hidden mystery, often referring to a powerful medicinal or alchemical secret)</note>; and *Anaxagoras* [had received] most or exceeding many. This appears by the disputations which he made of *Sol* <note>original: "Sol" (the Sun, often representing Gold in alchemical texts)</note> and the stone thereof, and which he left after his death, yet he was in many things contrary to the Egyptians; Wherefore even they would not be called Sophists nor Magi, but imitating *Pythagoras* in that thing they assumed the name of Philosophy; but yet they reaped no more than a few Glances like shadows, from the Magick of the Persians and Egyptians; But *Moses*, *Abraham*, *Solomon*, *Adam*, *Elias*, and the Magi that came from the East to Christ, were true Magi, and Divine Sophists, and Cabalists <note>term: Cabalist (a practitioner of the Kabbalah, a form of Jewish mysticism involving the study of divine emanations and the hidden meanings of scripture)</note>; which Art and Wisdom the Grecians knew very little of, or none at all; and therefore we shall leave that Philosophical Wisdom of the Grecians as a Speculation widely and largely distant, and separated from other true arts and sciences.

# CHAPTER IV.

## *What manner of Magi the Chaldeans, Persians, and Egyptians were.*

Many have attempted both to search out and bring to use the most secret Magic of those wise men; but yet it hath not hitherto been done. Many even in this our age do exalt *Trithemius* <note>Johannes Trithemius (1462–1516), a German abbot and influential cryptographer and occultist.</note>, others *Bacon* <note>Roger Bacon (c. 1219–1292), an English philosopher and friar associated with early experimental science and alchemy.</note> and *Agrippa* <note>Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa (1486–1535), author of 'Three Books of Occult Philosophy'.</note> for Magic and the Cabala <note>original: "Cabal"; refers to Kabbalah, a system of Jewish mysticism often adapted by Renaissance scholars into "Christian Cabala".</note>, which [two things] seem not in the least to agree, not knowing why they do so; Magic indeed is an art and faculty by which the Elementary bodies, and their fruits, their properties, virtues, and hidden <note>original: "obstruse"</note> operations are attained unto. But the Cabala, by a subtle understanding of the Scriptures seems to trace out the way for men to God, how to act with him, and prophesy from him; for the Cabala is full of Divine Mysteries, even as the Magic is full of natural secrets; for it teacheth presages and foretellings of or from the nature of things to come and things present; for its operation <note>original: "opparation"</note> consisteth in the knowing of the inner nature <note>original: "intrinsecality"</note> of all Creatures as well Celestial as Terrestrial bodies, [namely] what is hidden in them, what

# CHAPTER VI.

## *Of various Errors as to the matter, its finding out and knowledge.*

The Philosophers have prefixed various most hidden names to this matter of the stone, grounded on various similarities; Which *Arnoldus* <note>Arnold of Villanova (c. 1240–1311), a famous physician and alchemist.</note> observing, says in his *Rosary* <note>Referring to the *Rosarium Philosophorum*, a seminal alchemical text.</note>, that the greatest difficulty is to find out the matter of this stone; for they have called it Vegetable, Mineral, and Animal, not according to the literal sense, as is well known to such wise men as have tried the divine secrets and miracles of the same stone. For example's sake *Raymond Lully's Lanaria* <note>original: "Lanaria," likely referring to the "Sleeveless Campion" or "Soapwort," used here as a cryptic name for an alchemical substance.</note> may be produced, which gives flowers of admirable virtues familiar to the Philosophers themselves. But yet this was not the intention of those Philosophers, that you should think they meant thereby any <term>projection</term> <note>The final stage of alchemy where the Stone is cast upon base metals to transmute them into gold.</note> upon metals, or that any such preparation should thence be made; but the abstruse mind of the Philosophers has another intent; In like manner they called their matter by the name *Martagon* <note>A type of lily, used here as a symbolic name for a specific stage or substance in the work.</note>, to which they applied a hidden Alchemical operation,

### CHAP. VII.
#### *Of the Errors of those who seek the Stone from Vegetables, as mentioned above.*

Some of the Alchemists have expressed a juice out of *Celandine*, and boiled it to a thickness or consistency, and put it open to the Sun, that so it might be Coagulated by itself <note>original: "per se"</note> into a hard Mass, the which being afterwards beaten into a most small powder of a black or reddish-brown <note>original: "spadicious"</note> colour, should turn Mercury <note>Quicksilver</note> in projection into Gold <note>original: "Sol," the alchemical name for Gold</note>, the which they found to be also vain. Others have admixed Sal Ammoniac <term>Sal Ammoniac: Ammonium chloride, used as a flux in metalworking</term> to this powder, others the *Colcothar* <term>Colcothar: A reddish-brown iron oxide residue left after the distillation of vitriol</term> of Vi-

## CHAPTER VIII.
### Concerning those who have sought the *Stone* in *Animals*.

They have also, by a certain kind of symbolic resemblance, called this matter *Virgin’s Milk* <note>original: "Lac Virginis"; a common alchemical term for a white liquid or mercurial water</note>, and the blessed blood of a Rose-colored hue, which is nonetheless granted to [or enjoyed] only by the Prophets and the Sons of God. From this, the Sophisters <note>In this context, "Sophisters" refers to false alchemists or those who use deceptive reasoning</note> concluded that this Philosophical matter was contained in the blood of animals or of man; they took this opportunity also because animals are nourished by Vegetables. Others have sought it in hair, in the Salt of Urine, and *in Rebis* <note>original: "in Rebis"; likely referring here to human excrement or the "double thing" of the body</note>; others in Hen-eggs, and in milk, and in the Lime <term>Calx: a powder produced by heating a substance to high temperatures</term> of Eggshells, with all of which they thought they could fix Mercury. Some have extracted a Salt out of stinking urine, supposing that to have been the matter of the stone. There are even some persons who have judged the little stones found in *Rebis* to be the matter; others have soaked the membranes of eggs in a most sharp *Lye* <term>Lixivium: a strong alkaline solution or lye used in chemical extraction</term>, with which they have also mixed calcined eggshells as white as snow; to these they have attributed the

# The Aurora of Paracelsus.

*Electrum* <note>A natural or artificial alloy of gold and silver, often considered a base for further transmutation</note> into Gold; others (according to the *Venetians'* art, as they call it) take twenty lizard-like creatures, or more or less, and shut them in a pot, and make them even mad with hunger, so that they may devour one another, until only one of them survives. This survivor is then fed with the filings of Copper or *Electrum*, under the supposition that this animal will, by the digestion of its stomach alone, effect the wished-for transmutation. Last of all, they burn this animal into a red powder, which they thought must be Gold, but they were deceived. 

Others also, having burned the fishes called *Tratas* <note>likely trout; original: "Tratas"</note>, have by melting found sometimes some Gold in them; but there is no other reason for it but only this: namely, those fishes in rivers and brooks do sometimes meet with small scales and sparkles of Gold and eat them; yet it is but seldom. Such cheaters are to be found chiefly in the courts of Princes. 

[*Verily*] <insert>Truly</insert> the matter of the Philosophers is not to be sought after in animals; and of this I do admonish all. # The Aurora of Paracelsus.

<column-break/>

An obscure liquid began to sweat forth out of that matter in their vessel, drop by drop. From this occurrence, they <note>The "they" refers to the ancient alchemists or "Sages."</note> prophesied and foretold that in the final days, a most pure man would come upon the earth, by whom the redemption of the world should be accomplished. They believed that this same man would emit or send forth bloody drops of a rosy or red color, by which means he would redeem the world from sin. 

In a similar fashion, the "blood" of their stone—though according to its own specific nature—freed the <term>Leprous metals</term><note>In alchemy, "leprous" refers to base or "sick" metals like lead or iron that need to be "cured" into gold.</note> from their infirmities and corruption. For this reason, they believed they were justified in calling their stone "animal" <note>From the Latin 'animalis,' meaning 'having the breath of life' or 'soul.'</note>. Concerning this mystery, <term>Mercurius</term><note>Refers to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of alchemy.</note> speaks thus to King <term>Calid</term><note>Khalid ibn Yazid, an Umayyad prince associated with early alchemy.</note>: "This mystery is permitted only to the prophets of God to know, which is why this stone is called 'Animal.' For in the blood of this stone, its soul lies hidden." 

It is likewise composed of body, spirit, and soul. On the same account, they have also called it their <term>microcosm</term><note>A "little world" that encapsulates the entire universe in miniature.</note>, because it possesses the likeness of all things in the world; and thus they again called it "Animal," just as Plato called the great world <note>The Macrocosm or the universe at large.</note> an "Animal."

<summary>This page explains the alchemical analogy between the "Stone" and a living being, comparing the purification of metals to the spiritual redemption of humanity through a "pure man" and his blood. It justifies the term "animal" for the Stone by citing its tripartite nature of body, soul, and spirit.</summary>

# C H A P T E R  I X.

## Concerning those who have sought the Stone within Minerals.

Here we may add the many ignorant men who suppose the stone to be threefold, and to be hidden in three distinct lineages or kinds, *namely: Vegetable, Animal,* and *Mineral*. Because of this, they have sought for it specifically in *Minerals*. <note>Paracelsus is critiquing the "tria genera," a common alchemical theory that the Stone could be derived separately from plants, animals, or stones.</note> *<insert>Now</insert>* this opinion is far removed from the true opinion of the Philosophers; for they affirm that their stone is *uniformly Vegetable, Animal, and Mineral* all at once. Now, here you are to note that Nature has distributed its mineral sperm <note>In alchemy, "sperm" refers to the generative seed or essence of a substance.</note> into various kinds, *<insert>or subjects</insert> namely: into Sulphurs, Salts, Boraxes, Nitres, Ammoniac salts, Alums, Arsenics, Atramenta* <note>original: "Atramenta"; refers to black metallic dyes or vitriols.</note> or *Vitriols, Tutias, Hematites, Orpments, Realgars, Magnesias, Cinnabar, Antimony, Talc, Cachymia, Marcasites, etc.* <note>These terms represent the "middle minerals" or "spirits" used in the laboratory; for example, Tutia is zinc oxide, and Marcasite refers to various iron pyrites.</note> In all these, Nature has not yet attained to our true matter, although in some of the said species it reveals itself in a wonderful appearance for the transmutation of imperfect metals that are to be brought to perfection; for verily, a long experience,

<summary>Paracelsus argues that the Philosopher's Stone is a single substance combining vegetable, animal, and mineral properties, rather than being found in those categories separately. He lists various mineral substances that, while useful for experiments, do not yet constitute the true "matter" of the Stone.</summary>

## CHAPTER XI.

### *Of the true and perfect particular Secret of Arsenic, for the white Tincture.*

Some have written that Arsenic is compounded of Mercury and Sulphur; others of earth and water; and most say it is of the nature of Sulphur; But whatever matter it is of, the nature thereof is such, as to transmute Copper into whiteness <note>This refers to "whitening" copper to make it resemble silver, a common goal in "particular" or lesser alchemical works.</note>; Also it may be brought to that perfection of preparation, that it may truly and perfectly tinge <note>To "tinge" is to permanently change the color and nature of a metal through an alchemical tincture.</note>. # The Aurora of Paracelsus.

They have veiled over its <term>Arcanum</term> <note>The "Arcanum" refers to the secret, most potent medicinal essence of a substance.</note> with <term>enigmatic figures</term> <note>Symbolic or coded language used by alchemists to hide their secrets from the uninitiated.</note>, as thus, *viz.*

> Thou shalt visit the inward parts of the earth, and shalt find by rectification the occult stone, a true medicine; <note>This is a translation of the famous alchemical acronym VITRIOL: *Visita Interiora Terrae Rectificando Invenies Occultum Lapidem*.</note>

By the earth they understood the <term>Vitriol</term> <note>A sulfate mineral, often iron or copper sulfate, highly valued in alchemy for its transformative properties.</note> itself; and by the Inward part of the Earth, its sweetness and redness; because in the hidden part of the Vitriol, a subtle, noble, and most fragrant juice and pure oil lies hidden. The manner of drawing it forth is not at all to be attempted by <term>Calcination</term> <note>The process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile matter.</note> or distillation; for it must not at all be deprived of its greenness; for as soon as ever it is robbed thereof, its secret essence is also gone, and so necessarily it must lack its virtues. Truly it is to be observed here in this place, that not only the Minerals, but also the Vegetables themselves and such like, that outwardly show a <term>viridity</term> <note>Greenness or vitality.</note> or greenness, contain an Oil within them as red as blood, which is their secret essence. From this it is evident that the ridiculous distillations of the Apothecaries are vain and foolish and of no importance, because they do not at all know how to bring forth the blood-like redness of the Vegetables. Nature itself being wise, turns the waters of all Vegetables into a

<summary>Paracelsus explains that the true medicinal essence of Vitriol and plants is a hidden red oil found within their green exterior, which is lost if processed incorrectly by traditional apothecaries.</summary>
<keywords>Arcanum, Vitriol, Alchemy, Calcination, Distillation, Medicinal Essence, Paracelsus, Botany, Chemical Extraction</keywords>

--- Page 47 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of alchemical color changes, specifically the transition from green to red, following the previous section on the preparation of oils.</meta>

# The Aurora of Paracelsus.

A lemon-yellow color <note>original: "Citrine"</note>, and from there (afterwards) into a deep red oil like blood; Now the reason for this process occurring so slowly is the excessive haste of ignorant distillation operators, by which the greenness <note>original: "Viridity"</note> is consumed. They have not learned how to strengthen Nature in its inherent powers, by which the noble vigor [or greenness] <note>original: "virour"</note> ought to be refined into a redness by its own natural process <note>original: "per se"</note>. For the sake of example: white wine matures itself into a lemon-yellow color; and in the course of time, the green color of the grapes themselves is turned by its own nature into a red, which previously lay hidden under the sky-blue color. Therefore, when the greenness of the vegetables and minerals is lost through the laziness of the operators, their essence, the spirit of the oil, and the most noble balsam of the secret mysteries <term>Arcana: hidden medicinal or alchemical secrets</term> is also lost.

<summary>Paracelsus explains that the transition from green to red in alchemical oils must be achieved slowly to preserve the substance's "vigor," using the natural ripening of grapes as a metaphor. He warns that hasty distillation destroys the essential "balsam" or medicinal secret within the material.</summary>
<keywords>alchemical colors, distillation, greenness, citrine, red oil, balsam, Arcana, Paracelsus, maturation</keywords>

## Chapter XIII.
### Concerning the Process of Vitriol for the Red Tincture.

Vitriol <note>A historical term for sulfate minerals, most commonly green copperas or iron sulfate.</note> contains within itself many dirty and viscous <gloss>or slimy</gloss> imperfections; therefore, its greenness must be frequently extracted with water and rectified <note>The process of refining a substance through repeated distillation or purification.</note> until it has shed all the impurities of the earth. Once all these rectifications are finished, great care must be taken that the matter is not left exposed to the Sun; for the sunlight will turn its greenness into a pale hue and, at the same time, consume the Arcanum <note>The "secret" or essential healing/transformative essence of a substance.</note>. Let it be kept covered in a stove so that no contamination reaches it. Afterward, let it be digested <note>Subjected to gentle, continuous heat.</note> in a sealed glass vessel for several months, or until various colors appear, ending in the deepest redness. 

However, you must not assume that this process alone has sufficiently fixed the redness; it must be further purged of its internal, accidental earthly impurities in this manner: It is to be rectified with Acetum <note>Latin: vinegar or acetic acid.</note> until the earthly defilement is entirely removed.

<meta>The text continues on the next page with the conclusion of the word "removed."</meta>

<summary>This chapter details the purification of vitriol, emphasizing the need to protect the substance from sunlight to preserve its "Arcanum" while transitioning it from a green mineral to a red tincture.</summary>

## *The Animation is thus done.*

Take Venus <note>The alchemical name for Copper</note>, reduce it into thin Plates, as much as you will, ten, twenty, or forty pound; let them be incrusted or smeared over with a paste made only of Arsenic and calcined Tartar <note>Potassium tartrate, often derived from wine barrels and used as a flux</note>, and let them be calcined <note>Heated to a high temperature to induce chemical change</note> in their Vessel for the space of twenty-four hours; then at length let the Venus be beaten into powder, be washed and cleansed exceeding well. Let the Calcination be repeated, together with the washings, three or four times; By this way it becomes purged and cleansed from its gross greenness <note>original: "viridity," referring to the green oxidation of copper</note> and its unclean Sulphur; Verily you must beware of the Calcinations that are made with common Sulphur; for it does wholly corrupt all the good that is in a metal, and renders that which is evil worse. Now, to ten Marks <note>A traditional unit of weight for precious metals</note> of that purged Venus let there be added one of pure Lune <note>The alchemical name for Silver</note>; But so that by the projection <note>The final stage of the alchemical process where the "medicine" is cast upon the base metal to transform it</note> of the medicine the work may be the sooner accelerated and hastened, and that it may the more easily penetrate the imperfect body, and expel all such parts as are contrary to the nature of Lune, that very thing may be easily done by the

# The Aurora of Paracelsus.

By the continued heat of Sulphur <note>In alchemy, Sulphur represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle of change</note>, they cast off their vegetable nature and take on a mineral one. This happens primarily when the proper nourishment is taken away from these vegetables, whereby they are afterwards forced to take their nourishment from the sulphurs and salts of the earth. This continues until that which was previously a vegetable passes into a perfect mineral. 

From this mineral condition, a certain perfect metallic essence sometimes arises, and that occurs by progressing from one degree to another. But to return to the Stone of the Philosophers <note>The legendary "Philosopher's Stone" capable of turning base metals into gold and granting long life</note>, the matter of which (as some have mentioned) is the most difficult of all things to find and is hidden from the understanding. 

Now, the way and the most certain rule for finding this out—as well as discovering what all other things contain or are able to do—is a most diligent examination of their Root and Sperm <note>The "Sperm" refers to the primordial biological or metaphysical seed from which a substance grows</note>, through which knowledge is attained. # The Aurora of Paracelsus.

<margin>From Corporal <unclear>Melancholy</unclear> to the Best</margin>
It is exquisite, and most certainly known, that all things created by nature consist of three principles, namely of natural Sulphur, Mercury, and Salt, mixed into one, so that in some things they are Volatile <note>easily evaporated or changed</note>, and in other things fixed. As often as a corporal <note>physical or bodily</note> Salt is thoroughly mixed with a spiritual Mercury and Animated <note>endowed with life or soul</note> Sulphur into one body, then does nature begin to work in subterranean places (which serve as its vessels) by a separating fire. By this fire, the gross and impure Sulphur is separated from the pure, the Earth from the Salt, and the cloudiness from the Mercury. These purer parts being reserved, nature then boils them down <note>original: "decoct"</note> together into a pure Geogamic <note>term for "earth-born" or terrestrial</note> body. This operation is accounted by the Magi <note>wise men or practitioners of the high arts</note> as a mixture and conjunction by the union of the three: namely, body, soul, and spirit. This union being completed, there results from it a pure Mercury. If this Mercury flows through the subterranean passages and veins of the earth and meets with a Caheic <note>a Paracelsian term likely referring to a specific mineral or "harsh" quality</note> Sulphur, the Mercury is coagulated <note>thickened or solidified</note> by this Sulphur according to the specific condition of that Sulphur. But notwithstanding this, it is as of yet volatile,

From this arose the very common opinion that Mercury and Sulphur are the primary matter of metals, a view which is also supported by the reports of the Miners. Yet common mercury and common sulphur are not the true matter of metals; rather, the Mercury and Sulphur of the Philosophers <note>The "Philosophers" refers to the ancient and medieval alchemists who sought the hidden essence of matter.</note> are incorporated and innate within perfect metals and their forms, so that they never flee from the fire, nor are they corrupted by the power of the Elements. ### CHAPTER XVII.

## *Of the Preparation of the matter of the Philosophers stone.*

**T**his is that which nature doth most chiefly require, *namely* that its own Philosophical man <note>The "Philosophical man" refers to the essential human-like spirit or microcosm within the base matter</note> be brought into a Mercurial substance, that it may spring forth into the Philosophers stone. Moreover you are to note, that those common preparations of *Geber*, *Albertus Magnus*, *Thomas Aquinas*, *Rupescisca*, *Polidorus*, and such like <note>These are famous medieval alchemists and theologians whose works Paracelsus often critiqued as being too complex or misguided</note>, are nothing else but some particular Solutions, Sublimations and Calcinations, not at all pertaining to our Universal work, which work doth want only the most secret fire of the Philosophers; Therefore the fire and Azoth <note>Azoth: the universal solvent or "First Matter" of the soul, often symbolized by Mercury</note> may suffice thee; And whereas the Philosophers do make mention of some preparations, as of putrefaction <note>rotting or breaking down</note>, distillation, sublimation, calcination <note>reducing to ash by fire</note>, coagulation, dealbation <note>whitening</note>, rubification <note>reddening</note>, ceration <note>softening to a wax-like state</note>, fixation, and so forth, you are to understand, that in their Universal work Nature itself doth accomplish all the operations in the said matter, and not the

<summary>Paracelsus argues that the true preparation of the Philosophers' Stone relies on natural processes and a "secret fire" rather than the complex chemical procedures described by earlier medieval alchemists. He emphasizes that Nature itself performs the necessary transformations like whitening and reddening once the correct matter is found.</summary>

The **white** and the **red** <note>The *albedo* (whiteness) and *rubedo* (redness), the final two stages of the alchemical Great Work.</note> do proceed out of one root, without any medium. It is dissolved by itself, coupled by itself, whitens, and reddens; is made saffron-colored and black by itself, marries itself, and conceives in itself: It is therefore to be **decocted** <note>Heated or boiled to extract essence.</note>, to be baked, to be fused, it ascends, and descends. # The Aurora of Paracelsus.

<unclear>Arnoldus</unclear> <note>Likely referring to Arnold of Villanova, a famous 13th-century alchemist and physician.</note> speaks: If there be more or less earth added than nature can endure or bear, then the soul is choked, and no fruit is perceived from it, nor any fixation <note>The process of making a volatile substance stable under heat.</note>. The same thing must be considered regarding the water; namely, if more or less of it be taken, it will bring an equally inconvenient loss. For a superfluity of water will make the matter excessively moist, and a deficiency will render it drier and harder than is proper. If there be too much air, then there is an impression of too much tincture <note>The concentrated essence or "color" that transforms base metals into gold.</note>; if too little, then the body becomes pale. Likewise, if the fire be too vehement, the matter is burnt up; if it be too weak, it has not the power of drying, nor of dissolving, nor of heating the other elements. In these things does the elemental weight consist.

But the artificial weight is most occult <note>Hidden or secret.</note>; for it is enclosed within the magical art of ponderation <note>The precise measurement or weighing of substances.</note>. Now the philosophers say that between the spirit, soul, and body, the weight consists of sulphur as the guider of the work; for the soul greatly desires sulphur and must necessarily observe it in relation to the weight. Understand it

# *The Treasure of the Philosophers*

I could demonstrate to our modern physicians even the bare name only of the Conjunction <note>The chemical union of two substances</note>, Solution <note>The dissolving of a solid into liquid</note>, and Coagulation <note>The thickening or solidifying of a liquid</note>, which Nature itself in the beginning of the world has laid open and demonstrated in this its own created subject. A year would hardly suffice; no, nor all the paper that can be got would be enough to instruct or inform them. Verily, I say, that in this mineral there are three substances to be had, namely Mercury, Sulphur, and a mineral water, of which three it is composed. Being separated spagyrically <note>term: Spagyrically: The alchemical process of separating a substance into its components and then recombining them to purify it</note>, it is dissolved in its own proper and not yet ripe liquor, and is hidden as a pear in its proper tree. 

The hider of the pear is the tree, upon which as the stars and Nature do agree and work together, it gives forth green boughs, and afterwards in March yields buds, and manifests flowers, and so proceeds on even to the production of the fruit of the pear; then at Autumn the fruits depart or are ripe. Not unlike to this is it to be considered of as to the minerals in the bowels of the earth, which are thrust or brought forth by the *Astrums* <note>original: "Astrum" — referring to the celestial or "starry" influence that alchemists believed governed the growth of metals</note> or stars; which thing those alchemists must chiefly consider, that

# THE WATER-STONE

If therefore any one thinks to attain to that high and unspeakable mystery <note>The "Great Work" or *Magnum Opus* of alchemy, believed to be both a physical and spiritual transformation.</note>, let him know, that such an Art is not in the power of man, but consists in the most gracious will <insert>and pleasure</insert> of God, and that it is not the Will or Desire, but the mere Mercy of the Almighty that helps <insert>man</insert> thereunto: It is very expedient therefore, that <margin>Above all things</margin> thou beest Pious, and that thy heart be lifted up to him alone, and that thou askest that gift of him alone, by a true, most ardent, and undoubting praying; for from him alone it is to be obtained, and by him alone is given.

# The Water-stone

Therefore they have generally called it the **Stone of the Philosophers**; the most ancient, occult <note>hidden or secret</note>, unknown, natural, and incomprehensible [Stone;] yea they have called it the celestial, blessed, holy and **triune** <note>three-in-one</note> universal Stone of the wise men.

But now, the cause why they termed it a *stone*, and why they compared it to a *stone*, is (amongst other reasons) chiefly this; when the matter thereof [doth] at the beginning [come] like a mineral out of the earth, it is truly a stone; and then [again] because [this matter] is hard and dry, because also it is beaten and ground [to dust] like a stone, but especially [the reason is this] if it be divided into the three parts (which nature her self hath conjoined) then is it necessary that it itself be again digested <term>digested: a process of slow heating used to mature alchemical substances</term> in all the same [parts] and made of the nature of a constant **wax-like flowing stone**.

## *A Philosophical Enigma.*

IN which the first material subject of the Art of the wise men, (otherwise called the Phoenix <note>The Phoenix is a common alchemical symbol for the philosopher's stone, representing rebirth and purification through fire.</note> of the Philosophers) being wholly divided, is to be found in a threefold manner.

## *The Philosophical Enigma.*

IF I tell thee of the three parts of every thing, thou hast no cause to complain, for I tell thee the truth. Thou needest the three-leaved grass <note>Likely a reference to the shamrock or trefoil, used here as a metaphor for the alchemical trinity of Salt, Sulphur, and Mercury.</note>; sue to the Lord <note>original: "Jehovah"</note> by thy prayers. Seek for one in three, and thou shalt have one out of three. It is called by a thousand names; it is a body, soul and spirit; It is beautified with Salt, Sulphur and a heavy Mercury <note>The "Tria Prima" or three primary substances which alchemists believed composed all matter.</note>. Trust me, if thou understandest the three-leaved grass, and knowest the Voice and Song, then art thou a wise Artist <term>Artist: a practitioner of alchemy or the "Hermetic Art."</term>.

# Another Enigma much more plain.

There is one thing in this world, which is everywhere to be found; and that as it were accidentally or casually, without care; of a grayish and greenish colour, and of a wonderful power. In this thing is both a white and red colour <note>These colors represent the two main stages of the alchemical process: the Albedo (whiteness) and the Rubedo (redness).</note>; It flows hither like a swift stream, and runs away like a river; It wets not <note>A reference to "mercurial water," which was described as a liquid that does not dampen the hands.</note>, and is made of a heavy weight light. I could give it a thousand names, but thousands know it not: It is common to be seen, but the Art of it is difficult; He that dissolves it by a <term>medium</term> <note>A substance or environment acting as an intermediary to facilitate a chemical or spiritual change.</note>, and finishes it the third time, is a wise man, and rightly possesses this <term>noble subject</term> <note>The "Materia Prima" or the raw starting material required to create the Philosopher's Stone.</note>.

# Of the Wise Men

The substance that is prepared is light, thin, subtle, and soft; but the other is very heavy, firm, and hard. When these, therefore, are joined together in the Solutory Vessel <note>A vessel used for dissolving substances; from the Latin 'solvere', to loosen or dissolve.</note> [or <gloss>dissolving</gloss> dish] and are reduced as if into a dry liquid or Amalgama <note>A mixture or blend, usually of a metal with mercury.</note>, then leave them at first for six or seven days in a lukewarm heat, so that it may only have a kind of gentle warmth. 

Then again, take out one part of the three previously mentioned parts of the water, and put it in a round glass vessel (shaped like a phial or an egg) that has never been used before. Place the tempered liquid into the middle of it; and then again leave it so for six or seven days, and the body of Gold <term>☉</term> <note>The alchemical symbol for the Sun, representing Gold.</note> is dissolved by the water, little by little. 

This being done, here begins the Conjunction <note>The alchemical marriage or union of two opposite substances.</note> of these two, and there is such a sweet and delicate entrance of the one into the other, and such a mixing, as is like ice melting in warm water. And therefore the Philosophers have diversely described it, and have compared it to a Bridegroom and his Bride (just as Solomon has done in his Canticles <note>The Song of Solomon in the Bible, often interpreted by alchemists as a metaphor for chemical union.</note>).

# *Of the Wise Men*

For a Conclusion (for it is in no way advisable to pass over this lightly) observe that if by chance any misfortune or error, or any misunderstanding (which may easily happen, and so strongly hinder the perfection <note>The "perfection" refers to the successful completion of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>) should be committed or occur, then a timely remedy must be sought, and the evil or error must be corrected.

# An Enigma.

There are seven Cities, and seven metals <note>original: "met-tals"; likely referring to the seven planetary metals: Gold, Silver, Mercury, Copper, Iron, Tin, and Lead</note>, so counted.

Briefly, all things do quietly rest and prevail in this number.

# The process of the whole work is here briefly declared.

### *The first or former Labour.*
Dissolve the matter, and also Putrefy <note>To "putrefy" in alchemy refers to the breakdown of a substance through gentle heat, often resulting in a black color.</note> it:
Afterwards let it be distilled, and then
Coagulate <note>The process of turning a liquid into a solid or semi-solid state.</note> it.

### *The second or Latter Labour.*
Conjoin <note>To chemically or symbolically unite two substances, often referred to as the "chemical wedding."</note> two things; Putrefy them,
then blacken them:
Digest <note>To apply constant, gentle heat over a long period to mature the substance.</note> them till it become whitened by
thy Art.
Then at length rubify <note>To turn red; "rubification" is the final stage of the alchemical process, signifying the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> it to the highest;
Coagulation is a thing Profitable to this
Art; This done, fix <note>To "fix" a substance means to make it non-volatile, so it no longer evaporates or changes when exposed to heat.</note> it, and thou shalt be
a great man.
And if thou shalt (after all these
things) ferment this, thou hast luckily
finished the whole work of the Art, Circularly <note>Referring to the "Cyclical" nature of alchemy, where the end returns to the beginning, often symbolized by the Ouroboros.</note>.

# The Water-stone

Now in all those aforesaid things, it is found to be proved and experienced as perfect and infallible. Upon which account, **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the "Hermetic" alchemical writings are attributed.</note> and **Aristotle** call it—namely—true without lies, certain, indeed of all things the most certain, the secret of all secrets, of a divine efficacy, concealed and hidden from fools. Briefly, they have termed it the very utmost and chiefest thing that can be seen under heaven, and the wonderful epilogue and conclusion of all <term>Philosophical operations</term> <note>The various chemical and spiritual stages of the alchemical Great Work.</note>. 

Therefore some pious Philosophers are wholly of the opinion, that it was revealed from above to our first Parent, the first man **Adam**, and that it was sought for with a singular desire by all the holy Patriarchs. For it is said, that **Noah**, the framer of the Ark, and **Moses**, who made the Tabernacle and the Golden Vessels therein, and likewise **Solomon**, who to the glory of God finished the Temple and many other curious works and ornaments of all kinds, and did many other mighty great acts, did from thence obtain their long life and great riches. 

Even in like manner, the Philosophers will acknowledge the same: that by it they found out the <term>seven Liberal Sciences</term> <note>The classical curriculum of Grammar, Logic, Rhetoric, Arithmetic, Geometry, Music, and Astronomy.</note> and Arts, and that from thence they

# The Water-stone

Truly, one should consider and behold these matters far differently than is currently the custom of the world (the more is the pity). Most people today look upon these secrets with no more understanding than heifers or calves do. Furthermore, they seek after and covet the knowledge of this most noble <term>Art</term><note>Alchemy, often referred to as "The Art" or "The Royal Art" by practitioners.</note> out of avarice, luxury, pride, and for the sake of worldly honor and pleasure. In these intentions and thoughts, they err widely from the true goal. 

For God does not communicate such gifts to the wicked, nor to those who despise His Word, but only to the godly—those who lead an honest and quiet life in this evil and unclean world, who support themselves with integrity, and who reach out their helping hands to their needy neighbors. This is in accordance with the verses of the Poet, namely:

> *God gives the Art to honest men alone,*
> *Which all the world's gold cannot procure;*
> *It is not fit that the common crowd know it, for its*
> *wickedness; it seeks for this stone in vain:*
> *He who possesses it in secret, may reside*
> *wherever he pleases.*

# The Water Stone

Regarding its other qualities and profitable use, such as daily experience brings before our eyes (part of which I myself have been made a participant in through Divine grace), I shall here add a few things.

First of all: As to what pertains to the knowledge of God, as well as those miracles of nature which have been revealed through the benefit of that Art <note>The "Art" refers to Alchemy, viewed here as a sacred science</note>, I cannot sufficiently describe or declare it according to its supreme dignity. For in this, a man shall be able most elegantly to see—as if it were painted in a mirror—the image of the most holy Trinity in one Divine, indissoluble Essence; he may see how the Trinity differs and yet is, notwithstanding, only one God. 

Furthermore, one may see the second person of the Deity <note>Jesus Christ</note> concerning his taking on of human flesh, his birth, passion, death, and resurrection. Likewise, one may see his exaltation and that eternal happiness earned by his death for us men, his creatures. Moreover, there may also be seen the purification of Original Sin through the obtained mediums <note>original: "mediums"; refers to the intermediary substances or processes in alchemy that mirror spiritual salvation</note>, without which the counsels and actions of all men, and all their works, are in vain and worth nothing. Briefly, all the articles of the Christian faith, and the

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of divine communication, specifically focusing on the use of parables and types as pedagogical tools used by God throughout biblical history.</meta>

# Of the Wise Men

Parables in this earthly life are daily obvious to our eyes, and are as it were painted out for us, and set before us; for example's sake, when God (in *Genesis* 3) intended to show to *Adam* in Paradise after his Fall his punishment, namely Mortality and corporal death, it pleased him to signify it in the following manner: that whereas the earth has not by itself any life at all, and whereas he was taken and framed out of the earth, therefore also must he be made like the earth again. <note>A reference to the biblical decree "dust thou art, and unto dust shalt thou return."</note>

So in the 15th and 23rd chapters of *Genesis*, when God would discover to *Abraham* the increase of his Seed and Family, he bade him to behold the Stars in the Heaven, the Sand in the Sea, and the dust of the earth, as a <term>Type</term> <note>A "type" is a person, event, or object in the Old Testament believed to foreshadow something in the New Testament or a spiritual reality.</note>. Such like various, very pleasant, and sweet Typical Prefigurations God commanded his Prophets to propose and demonstrate to his People *Israel*, when he would announce [or declare] any singular thing unto them. 

This Christ himself likewise, who is the mouth and foundation of truth, did do in his Testament, and proposed all things in Parables, that so his Doctrine might be the better understood; as for example, when he would hint unto us—

<summary>This page explains how God uses physical parables and "types"—such as Adam's connection to the earth or Abraham's view of the stars—to communicate spiritual truths and future promises to humanity.</summary>

# Of the Wise Men

This is to be done in the <term>Theological work</term><note>The author uses "work" here in the sense of the alchemical "Great Work" (Magnum Opus), but applied to the soul's salvation.</note>; for as soon as the conjunction and spiritual union of Man with Christ is made in Baptism, and we are thus reduced (as we said before) into one body with him, so soon also [or, then] must such a man learn the Christian faith by little and little, and comprehend one Article after another, until at length he be altogether confirmed in them, and attain to a perfect knowledge.

Therefore also the Christian faith (referencing Philippians 3) is—just as is done with the <term>earthly watery prepared matter</term><note>A reference to the "Prima Materia" or the initial substance used in alchemy, which must be carefully processed.</note>, of which we have made mention in its proper place—divided into twelve Particles, or short Articles, according to the number of the 12 Apostles, and further is then again distributed into 3 principal Articles:

1. Concerning our Creation.
2. Our Redemption.
3. Our Sanctification.

It is expedient that a man propose to himself to learn these one after the other. But yet it must be done by little and little, and at various [distinct] times [and seasons], lest he should be burdened more than is fit, and be (as it were) wholly overwhelmed with his learning of them, by which he might perhaps become a loather thereof.

# The Water-stone

The new regeneration begun in baptism is only the start of this second right; and now, (first of all) this perfect and new regeneration into eternal life shall quicken and raise us up to that new and never-fading life. # The Epilogue or Conclusion.

Thus have you, my friend and courteous Reader, a brief and simple demonstration and declaration, and (along with it) an infallible counterpoise <note>A balancing weight or comparison</note>, and allegorical comparison of the earthly Chemical <note>original: "terrene Chymical"</note>, and the true Celestial stone Jesus Christ, by whom you may attain to a certain happiness and perfection, not only here in this earthly life, but also in the life eternal: Now although this comparison might have been more accurately (perhaps) and more copiously handled in the aforementioned Theological work, yet you are to know, that I am no Professor [or Teacher] of the Scripture, or any Aristotelian Theologian <note>A scholar who uses Aristotle’s logic to explain divinity</note> [or Divine] according as the custom of the World is nowadays; but that I am a citizen and a private person; for, as for that knowledge granted to me by God, I obtained it not by study in any of their famous Academies, or Universities, but in the Universal School of nature, (Job 12 <note>A reference to Job 12:7-8: "But ask the animals, and they will teach you..."</note>) and out of the great book of Miracles (in which all the God-learned did for many ages
past

# The Water-stone

past exercise themselves) did I likewise <note>The author refers to his formative years or previous spiritual practices.</note> learn. And therefore I have directed that description of mine, not according to the decked and as it were, soothed half, or lofty letter [and expression] (as I said before) but according to simplicity and plainness. Besides, it is not my office [or function] to set down here any more plentiful and larger treatise [or Commentary] about Theological things; but that which I have done, I did it (as much as concerns me) for such as have not yet made such a large progress; to them was I willing to prescribe some short delineation, whereby they may make a higher search thereinto. For it seems to be the duty of every lover of the truth by no means slightly to pass over the wonders of God, nor to wrap them in a perpetual silence, but to celebrate, amplify and magnify them. Moreover, I could willingly make a public confession of my faith, *namely* what I think or believe of the Articles of the Christian Religion. # The Water-stone

I answer, that as for such men, I shall leave them as I find them, and shall not here make any disputation as to where and how they obtained that same <term>tincture</term><note>In alchemy, a "tincture" is a potent medicinal or transformative substance, often the goal of the Great Work, capable of "tinging" or changing the properties of other bodies.</note>; But, that they themselves made and prepared the true and right tincture which I have here spoken of in all this treatise of mine, that no body (verily) shall persuade me of, much less can I be induced to believe it; especially considering that tragical event into which those kind of vain men do (with the tincture that they have) precipitate themselves; testimonies whereof, and such kinds of examples we find (the more is the grief) even now to this very day. 

I shall omit to speak how the <term>Chemical Art</term><note>The "Chymick Art" refers to the broader practice of chemistry and alchemy before they were distinct disciplines.</note>, together with its appurtenances, is not only one, but is diverse; And even as in other faculties there are diverse and mutable sectaries [or such whom others follow] to be found, as in Genesis 30: *But Jacob took the rods or sticks, etc.* <note>A reference to the biblical story where Jacob used peeled rods to influence the breeding of Laban's livestock, here used as an analogy for different methods or "rods" used by practitioners.</note> So also is it in this art. They are all generally called Chemists, but yet are not all informed and directed after one and the same intention or scope.

But I do here speak only of the true artificial Alchemy, as is agreeable to nature, the which doth especially teach, namely: how

<summary>The author distinguishes between true alchemists and "vain men" who possess false tinctures, arguing that genuine alchemy must follow the laws of nature. He uses a biblical analogy from Genesis to illustrate that while many claim the title of chemist, their methods and goals differ greatly.</summary>

# 222.
## The Water-stone

Therefore, if there be some one or other of those kind of chemists <note>original: "Chymists"; early modern practitioners of alchemy and chemistry</note> that you may meet with (who brag of the true chemical art, and of such an art as is agreeable to nature, and would willingly teach it to you, or any other person, for the sake of money, and pretend that they themselves are not able to pay the costs and charges that belong to it) then be faithfully warned that you do not trust such men too much; for most times there lies a snake in the grass. (Micah 2) <note>A reference to the biblical prophet Micah, chapter 2, regarding those who devise iniquity.</note> *If I should be of an erring spirit, etc.* <note>original: "Si spiritu errans..."</note>

Besides, I can truly affirm, that all the cost which perhaps is to be spent on the whole universal work <note>The "Great Work" or the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note> (setting aside the daily food and the fuel for sustaining the fire) does not exceed the price of 3 Florins <note>A gold or silver coin used throughout Europe; the author emphasizes that the raw materials are nearly worthless.</note>; for the matter (as we have heard above) is partly vile [or lowly] and by reason of that its vileness, is partly contemptible, as it were, and is everywhere to be found more than enough for the supply of your necessity, and that without much trouble. So also the labor is easy and not very laborious or painful. Briefly, the whole Art is most simply and most easily understood by the pious, and by such as are chosen by

<meta>catchword: God</meta>

# The Appendix.

Know likewise, that if (by reason of that gift <note>The "gift" refers to the successful creation of the Philosopher's Stone or the mastery of alchemy.</note> vouchsafed thee by God) thou happen to grow proud, or to be covetous under the cover or excuse of a provident care of thy family and sparingness <note>thrift or frugality</note>, and dost hereby tempt thyself to a turning away from God by little and little, then know (for I speak the truth) that that <term>Art</term> <note>The practice of alchemy and the ability to perform transmutations.</note> will vanish from under thy hands, insomuch that thou shalt not know how thou didst it. The which thing verily hath befallen more than one beyond their expectation.

<summary>This appendix serves as a moral warning to the alchemist, stating that the divine gift of the Art will be lost if the practitioner falls into the sins of pride or greed.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, morality, pride, greed, divine gift, spiritual warning</keywords>

*Reeves' Sermons Entitled the Strait-Gate*, in large octavo.

Several pieces by *H. N.* <note>Hendrik Niclaes, founder of the "Family of Love" sect</note> in octavo, namely, *Prophecy of the Spirit of Love*. *The Revelation of God* <note>original: "Revelatio Dei"</note>.

*Introduction to the Glass of Righteousness*. *The Gospel of the Kingdom* <note>original: "Evangelium Regni"</note>, a joyful message of the Kingdom, *Spiritual Tabernacle*. The *first Exhortation*. The *Apology for the service of Love*, all in octavo.

<margin>+</margin>*Samuel Hartlib’s Chemical addresses* <note>Hartlib was a key figure in the scientific revolution and a friend of Milton</note>, in octavo.

*Thomas Butler* his *little Bible of the Man*, or the *book of God opened in Man*, in octavo.

*Crooked Paths made straight*, or the ways of God made known in lost sinners, by *A. Yeomans*, in octavo.

*Lawrence’s Gospel separation separated from abuses*.

*Simmons' Saints like Christ*, in octavo.

*William Sedgwick* his eleven Sermons, *Entitled Some Flashes of Lightnings of the Son of Man*, in octavo.
